Let's design final program carefully:
- Use FFT convolution for score and gradients.
- Main objective: We can optimize score via minimizing a smooth approximation S_τ(p)=2n * (1/τ) log∑ exp(τ (b_k - shift))? Since scale invariant, ignoring constant 2n. We can use LSE on b_k. For sequence p sum1. But b can be very small; use offset max? Compute lse(β b) - β max? Gradient weights=softmax(β(b - max)) perhaps. For τ large, w on peaks. We can implement projected gradient with adaptive τ and line search as before but with better line search (brent?).

- Implement function objective_and_grad(p, beta): computes b full, max, lse (stable), weights, gradient w.r.t log-sum-exp. For maximizing? We minimize Φ=MX? Actually if p sum1, score=2n max b. Smooth approximate min logsumexp(β b). But note lse(β b) grows with scale? p fixed sum. Gradient = (1/β?)? Let Z=∑e^{β b}. d/dp logZ = β ∂b/∂p weighted. For projected gradient, direction = weighted average of b gradients times β; step should scale 1/β? We can instead use weights softmax(β(b-max)) and minimize weighted sum ∑w b. Gradient = weighted average of b gradients (without β). This is equivalent; step not scale. Use weights concentrated on peaks. Previous did this. Could use full softmax not just near max? If β small, weights spread, trying to reduce average (which constant) not helpful; but can flatten. Use adaptive τ.

- For local polish, after descent, use exact active set: Let A be indices with b_k ≥ max - ε (relative). We want move p to reduce weighted sum over A. We can solve a QP minimizing ∑_{k∈A} b_k subject sum=1, p≥0. Although Q matrix may not be PSD, but for small active set maybe can use scipy.optimize.minimize on low dimension? Or use projected gradient with weights uniformly over A.

- Use conditional gradient (Frank-Wolfe) on subgradient: direction is a vertex? Already similar.

- Use line search: After direction d (normalized?), try a few step sizes 0.01..1 on simplex using interpolation: p_step = project(p + α d) or geodesic (1-α)p+α q. The state-of-art used convex combination with q from LP. We can combine p with a candidate q that reduces peaks.

- Implement LP direction (SOTA) as optional: given p and M, solve maximize sum g s.t. g≥0, p*g≤M. This can be expensive but n=128/256 okay maybe every few iterations? We can use scipy.optimize.linprog. However this LP finder may be slow but can produce excellent directions. Let's derive properly for objective. Let p length n, M=max(p*p). We want q≥0 such that p*q≤M (componentwise) and maximize sum q. Since p is feasible and sum=1. This LP has 2n-1 constraints, n vars. If sum_q >1, rescale q to sum1. Then use q as direction. But is it guaranteed descent? The state-of-art says yes. We can use it as a restart/polish every so often for small n. Need implement efficiently. But linear constraints p convolved with variable q; coefficients for constraint k: for each j, p_{k-j}. This matrix size (2n-1)x n. Linprog highsl maybe. For n=128, 255 constraints, 128 vars; okay. Use sparse. Objective c=-ones. Bounds (0,None). rhs=M. At optimal, sum_q maybe >1. If sum_q<=1, direction just uniform? We can set q = best point maybe p. Then combine p ← (1-t)p + t q_rescaled. Could try t=0.1,0.2,... choose best actual score. This can be used as a mutation.

But perhaps the LP direction is exactly gradient of some dual and could be powerful. We can implement and call for best sequence during polish. But 2400s maybe plenty. However scipy linprog with (highs) for each call may take 0.1-1s for n=128; we can't call too often. Use a few times.

7. Meta-search of continuous param functions:
Define functions to generate p from parameters:
- `generate_poly`: p_i ∝ exp(∑_{d=0}^D C_d x_i^d) with C random constrained nonnegative. Sample D up to 6. This is flexible positive. But exp can blow; clip/normalize. We can random C with small variance to keep nonnegative. Evaluate score (no optimize) and choose best starts. Then run local.
- `generate_mixture_gaussians`: p_i ∝ ∑_{r=1}^R w_r exp(-0.5((x-μ_r)/σ_r) + skew_r * (x-μ_r)). Nonnegative. R=2-4. This can make asymmetric bumps. Use log-sum-exp for stability.
- `generate_piecewise_linear`: R knots with heights positive, linearly interpolate; R=2-8. This can approximate sharp shapes. Points x_j random sorted, heights random lognormal. Ensure endpoints maybe positive/zero.
- `generate_sigmoid`: p_i∝1 + a*sigmoid((x-c)/s) + b*... Could be included in piecewise.
- Use previous height_sequence_1.
- Uniform, random, two-cluster, U-shape.

We can evaluate each candidate cheaply with n (e.g., 256) and keep top diverse. Then run local optimizers with time split.

def smooth_weights(b, gamma):
    x = gamma*(b - b.max())
    # stable
    x -= x.max()
    w=np.exp(x); s=w.sum(); if s<.. onehot
    return w/s

   z -= dz
   z -= z.mean()
   p=softmax(z)
```

   - # direction scale ~1; choose alpha list. Note z - alpha direction.

Need implement `softmax_z` stable:
```
def softmax_z(z):
    z_shift = z - np.max(z)
    p = np.exp(z_shift)
    s = p.sum()
    if not finite: fallback
    return p/s
```
When z has large spread, exp underflows to 0 but p sum maybe ok if max. If s=0, set uniform.

LP implementation detail:
```
def lp_direction(p):
    from scipy.optimize import linprog
    from scipy.sparse import csr_matrix
    p = np.asarray(p,float)
    if p.size < 8: return None
    # floor p for feasibility
    p_lp = p + 1e-10
    p_lp /= p_lp.sum()
    b = full_conv(p_lp); M = max(b) + 1e-12
    n=p.size
    rows=[]; cols=[]; vals=[]
    for j in range(n):
        # k from j to j+n-1 inclusive
        ks = j + np.arange(n)
        rows.append(ks)
        cols.append(np.full(n, j))
        vals.append(p_lp[ks - j]) # p_lp[0..n-1]
    A = csr_matrix((vals, rows, cols), shape=(2*n-1,n))
    c = -np.ones(n)
    res = linprog(c, A_ub=A, b_ub=M, bounds=(0,None), method='highs', options={'time_limit': 60})
    if res.status==0 and res.x is not None:
       q=np.maximum(res.x,0); s=q.sum(); if s>1e-10: return q/s
    return None
```

Example:
```
def generate_starts(n, rng, prev=None, count=40):
    starts=[]
    # fixed
    starts.append(np.ones(n))
    x=np.linspace(0,1,n)
    starts.append(1-x + 0.1)
    starts.append(x +0.1)
    starts.append(1/(1-x+1e-3))
    starts.append(1/(1+np.exp(3*(x-0.4))))
    # random power
    for _ in range(count//2):
       alpha=rng.uniform(-0.5,3); beta=rng.uniform(-0.5,3)
       a=(x+1e-6)**beta*(1-x+1e-6)**alpha + 1e-3
       starts.append(a)
    # random sigmoid mixture
    for _ in range(count//3):
       c=rng.uniform(0.1,0.9); s=rng.uniform(0.05,0.4); h=rng.uniform(0.5,5); sign=rng.choice([-1,1])
       a=1 + sign*h/(1+np.exp((x-c)/s))
       a=np.maximum(a,1e-6)+...
    # random gauss mixture
    for _ in range(count//3): comps=1+rng.integers(0,3); heights lognormal; means beta; sigma .1-.5
    # random lowpass Fourier
    for _ in range(count//4): modes=3+rng.integers(0,6); amplitudes normal; z=sum a cos + b sin; a=exp(0.5*z) maybe.
    # spline
    for _ in range(count//4): knots random
    # prev interpolated if available
    # random full smooth
    for _ in range(count//4): z=rng.normal(n); apply lowpass via np.fft? Could just smooth with cumulative?
```

Detailed helper functions:
```
def full_conv(a):
    n=a.size
    size=2*n-1
    fsize=1 << (size-1).bit_length()
    fa=np.fft.rfft(a, fsize)
    b=np.fft.irfft(fa*fa, fsize)[:size]
    b = np.ascontiguousarray(b)
    # clean tiny negatives
    b = np.where(b < 0.0, 0.0, b) # but if negative large? no
    return b
```
If a has tiny negative? We ensure nonneg. If b sum not 1 due normalization, okay.

`score_and_b(p)`:
```
b=full_conv(p)
sc=2.0*len(p)*float(np.max(b)) if b.size else inf
return sc,b
```

`normalize_nonneg(a)`:
```
a=np.asarray(a,float)
a=np.maximum(a,0)
s=a.sum()
if not finite or s<=1e-300: a=np.ones_like(a); s=a.sum()
a=a/s
return a
```
But for starts with floor, don't normalize? We'll call.

`project_simplex(v)` (for random perturb maybe not needed in log domain but LP combine uses convex combination sum1, no project needed; previous random perturb may need). Implement for completeness.

`softmax_z(z)`: stable.
```
def softmax_z(z):
    zm = z - np.max(z)
    # if max large? zm finite. exp
    p = np.exp(np.clip(zm, -50, 50))
    s=p.sum()
    if s<=0: p=np.ones_like(z)/z.size
    return p/s
```
Clipping at 50 may distort if z spread >50; but if z spread >50, p effectively one-hot; clipping okay? It could artificially give mass to all if clipped to -50 (1e-22) negligible. Good.

`log_of_p(p)`: `np.log(np.maximum(p,1e-300))`; subtract mean.

`optimize_logadam(p0, budget, rng, verbose=False, focus_max=True)`:
Need handle time zero.
Pseudo:
```
def optimize_logadam(p0, budget, rng, gamma_start=5.0, gamma_end=200.0):
    p=normalize_nonneg(p0)
    n=p.size
    z=np.log(p+1e-300); z-=z.mean()
    m=np.zeros(n); v=np.zeros(n)
    b1=0.9; b2=0.999; eps=1e-6
    it=0; last_improve=0; stall=0
    best_p=p.copy(); best_sc=score_and_b(p)[0]
    z_best=z.copy()
    current=best_sc
    gamma=gamma_start
    alphas=[2.0,1.0,0.5,0.25,0.1,0.05]
    # If n large, fewer alphas
    if n>512: alphas=[1.0,0.5,0.2,0.1]
    while budget>0 and time<deadline...:
       it+=1
       sc,b=score_and_b(p); current=sc
       if sc<best_sc: update best, z_best; last_improve=it
       # schedule gamma based on it
       frac=min(1.0, it/20000.0)
       gamma = gamma_start + (gamma_end-gamma_start)*frac + (rng.random()-0.5)*gamma*0.2
       gamma=max(1,gamma)
       # weights
       maxb=b.max()
       shift=gamma*(b-maxb)
       shift-=shift.max()  # should <=0; after subtract max =0
       w=np.exp(np.clip(shift,-50,0))
       ws=w.sum(); if ws<1e-300: onehot at max; else w/=ws
       gp=2.0*n*np.correlate(w,p,mode='valid')
       mean_b=1.0/(2*n-1)
       r=b-mean_b
       gv=2.0*n*np.correlate(r,p,mode='valid')
       # scale normalize
       ngp=np.linalg.norm(gp); ngv=np.linalg.norm(gv)
       if ngp>1e-12: gp/=ngp
       if ngv>1e-12: gv/=ngv
       if current > 1.52: vw=0.4
       elif current > 1.48: vw=0.2
       else: vw=0.0
       # if current very high >1.8 vw=0.6
       if current > 1.8: vw=0.6
       G = (1-vw)*gp + vw*gv
       # Could add tiny noise
       if rng.random()<0.1: G += 0.1*rng.standard_normal(n)
       gp_dot=np.dot(p,G)
       zgrad = p*(G - gp_dot)
       # sum zero
       zgrad -= zgrad.mean()
       itad=it
       m=b1*m+(1-b1)*zgrad
       v=b2*v+(1-b2)*zgrad*zgrad
       mh=m/(1-b1**itad)
       vh=v/(1-b2**itad)
       direction=mh/(np.sqrt(vh)+1e-6)
       direction=np.clip(direction,-10,10)
       direction-=direction.mean()
       # line search
       best_candidate_sc=sc; best_candidate_z=z; accepted_current=False
       # maybe include random direction small? no
       for alpha in alphas:
           zn=z-alpha*direction
           pn=softmax_z(zn)
           scn,_=score_and_b(pn)
           if scn < best_candidate_sc:
                best_candidate_sc=scn; best_candidate_z=zn; best_candidate_p=pn
       # accept logic
       if best_candidate_sc < sc:
           z=best_candidate_z; p=best_candidate_p; stall=0
       else:
           # anneal accept maybe
           if rng.random() < 0.02:
              alpha=alphas[-1]
              zn=z-alpha*direction
              pn=softmax_z(zn)
              scn,_=score_and_b(pn)
              if scn < sc*1.01:
                  z=zn; p=pn
           # maybe if long no improve, reheat partially
       if it-last_improve > 1000: # maybe threshold adaptive
           # If many iters no improve, reheat from best
           if it-last_improve > max(2000, 0.1*it):
               scale = 0.05 + 0.15*rng.random()
               z = z_best + scale*rng.standard_normal(n)
               z -= z.mean()
               p = softmax_z(z)
               m.fill(0); v.fill(0)
               gamma=gamma_start
               last_improve=it
               # maybe current recomputed next loop
       if it % 10000 ==0:
           # reheat gamma
           gamma = gamma_start + rng.random()*(gamma_end-gamma_start)
    return p, best_sc
```

Need correct FFT for c_i=∑ p_m w_{i+m}. Let's derive with correlation via convolution using reversed arrays.
We want c_i = ∑_{m=0}^{n-1} p_m w_{i+m}, i=0..n-1, w length M=2n-2 (indices 0..M).
This is cross-correlation. Using FFT: circular correlation of P and W: C_j = ∑_{m} p_m w_{(m+j) mod L}, where if L large enough and j from 0 to n-1, no wrap because m+j ≤ n-1 + (n-1)=2n-2=M. Set L≥M+1=2n-1? Actually p length n, w length M; m+j max 2n-2 = M, so L=2n-1 sufficient (power two). If we perform circular correlation with L>=2n-1, C_j for j=0..n-1 equals desired. Great. How to compute circular correlation? For arrays p and w, C_j = ∑ p_m w_{m+j}. In numpy, `np.correlate(w, p, 'valid')` gives exactly C_j? Let's verify small: p=[p0,p1], w length3 [w0,w1,w2]. np.correlate(w,p,mode='valid') length n=2. Formula numpy: c_k = ∑_n a_{n+k} * conj(v_n) for k=0..len(a)-len(v). a=w len3, v=p len2: c0=w0*p0 + w1*p1; c1=w1*p0 + w2*p1? That is ∑ p_m? For i=0: want p0 w0 + p1 w1 => c0 yes. For i=1: want p0 w1 + p1 w2 => c1 yes. So `np.correlate(w,p,'valid')` desired.

FFT: Circular correlation same as convolution with reversed p? C_j = ∑ p_m w_{m+j}. Let q_m = p_{n-1-m} (reverse p). Convolution q * w: h_k = ∑_{m} q_m w_{k-m}. For k=n-1+j: h=∑_{m=0}^{n-1} p_{n-1-m} w_{n-1+j-m}. Let r=n-1-m => m=n-1-r; w_{j+r}; sum p_r w_{j+r}=C_j. So C is coefficients of convolution q*w at indices n-1+j. We can compute full conv via FFT: L >= (n-1)+(2n-2)+1 =3n-2? Wait q length n, w length 2n-1, full conv length 3n-2. But because we only need middle n coefficients, circular with L maybe? If L >= (n-1)+M+1 =3n-2 to avoid wrap. However earlier circular correlation with L=2n-1 may work if using DFT product conj(P)*W, not convolution q*w. Let's use DFT correlation formula: If L >= n+M? For circular c_j =∑ p_m w_{m+j mod L}. Need no wrap for j 0..n-1 and m 0..n-1. If L >= n-1 + M? Condition m+j < L for all. Max m+j=2n-2. So L>=2n-1 enough! Wait m max n-1, j max n-1 -> 2n-2. w index M=2n-2. So L=2n-1. Then yes, no wrap. Circular correlation via DFT: C = ifft(conj(fft(p,L)) * fft(w,L)) gives C_j =∑ p_m w_{m+j mod L}. Need check conjugation orientation. In signal, cross-correlation a*b = ifft(conj(A)*B) gives ∑ a_m b_{m+k}. Let's test with small using DFT property? For L, ifft_j(∑_m conj(P_m?) Wait DFT: A_ω=∑ p_m e^{-iωm}, B_ω=∑ w_m e^{-iωm}. Product conj(A)B = ∑ p_m e^{iωm} w_l e^{-iωl}. Inverse e^{iωj}/L -> coefficient when l = m+j? Because e^{iω(m-l+j)}, index j = l-m. So gives ∑ p_m w_{m+j}. Yes desired. Good. So we can compute C for j=0..n-1 using L=pow2 >=2n-1. Great! No need 3n. Because w length M+1=2n-1, p length n, circular shift. Condition l=m+j ≤2n-2 within L (2n-1). Excellent.

Thus we can implement FFT gradient:
```
def correlate_valid_fft(w,p):
    n=p.size
    L=1 << (2*n-1 -1).bit_length()  # >=2n-1
    fw=np.fft.rfft(w,n=L)
    fp=np.fft.rfft(p,n=L)
    c=np.fft.irfft(np.conj(fp)*fw,n=L)[:2*n-1]
    return c[:n]
```
Let's test mentally n=2, L>=3 pow2=4. p=[p0,p1], w len3. DFT should give c0=w0p0+w1p1, c1=w1p0+w2p1. Good. Need real; small imaginary.

This reduces gradient to O(n log n). Great. Use this in optimizer.

Full_conv uses Lfc=next power >=2n-1; same L. We can maybe share L? For a*a convolution length 2n-1 uses L pow2 >=2n-1. Same. We can use one L for both. In score_and_b, full_conv. In gradient, same L. Could optimize by storing L, but function side effects. Simpler compute in correlate.

Note for full convolution a*a, circular with L>=2n-1: `ifft(F*F)` length 2n-1 gives linear convolution because degree 2n-2 < L. For correlation, using L>=2n-1 also works because max shift 2n-2. Good.

Implement `gradient_from_weights_p(w,p)` using FFT. For var gradient, same with r. This will speed n=2048.

Need ensure w and r length 2n-1. `np.fft.rfft(w, L)` works.

```
def lp_polish(p, budget, rng):
    if not HAVE_SCIPY or p.size<=8: return p, score
    best=p; bestsc=score
    t0=time
    attempts=0
    attempts maybe 3 while time rem>20
    while time < t0+budget and attempts<5:
       q=lp_direction(p)
       if q is None: break
       for t in [0.1,0.2,0.4,0.6,0.8]:
           cand=normalize_nonneg((1-t)*best + t*q)
           sc=score_simplex(cand)
           if sc<bestsc: best=cand; bestsc=sc
       p = best
       attempts++
       # short optimize after improvement
       if time < t0+budget-5: p,bestsc = optimize_logadam(p, min(20, remaining), rng)
    return best,bestsc
```

def start_power(n, rng):
    x=(np.arange(n)+0.5)/n
    alpha=rng.uniform(-0.5,3.0)
    beta=rng.uniform(-0.5,3.0)
    a=np.power(x+1e-6,beta)*np.power(1.0-x+1e-6,alpha)+1e-5
    return a

def generate_starts(n, rng, count=40, prev=None):
    starts=[]
    x=np.linspace(0.0,1.0,n)
    starts.append(np.ones(n))
    starts.append(1.0-x+0.05)
    starts.append(x+0.05)
    starts.append(1.0/(0.5+x))
    starts.append(1.0/(0.5+ (1.0-x)))
    # fixed sigmoids
    for c,s,h in [(0.3,0.2,2.0),(0.6,0.2,-2.0),(0.5,0.1,3.0),(0.5,0.3,-3.0)]: ...
    # previous interpolation
    if prev is not None:
       p=normalize_nonneg(prev)
       if p.size == n: starts.append(p.copy())
       else: starts.append(interp_sequence(p,n))
    # random count
    for _ in range(count):
       typ=int(rng.integers(0,6))
       if typ==0: starts.append(start_power(n,rng))
       elif typ==1: starts.append(start_sigmoid_mix(n,rng))
       elif typ==2: starts.append(start_gauss_mix(n,rng))
       elif typ==3: starts.append(start_fourier(n,rng))
       elif typ==4: starts.append(start_spline(n,rng))
       else: starts.append(start_poly(n,rng))
    return starts
```
Fixed sigmoids maybe duplicates.

`interp_sequence` top-level:
```
def interp_sequence(p,n):
    p=np.maximum(np.asarray(p,float),0)
    if p.size==n: return normalize_nonneg(p)
    if p.size==1: return np.full(n,p[0])
    xold=np.linspace(0,1,p.size)
    xnew=np.linspace(0,1,n)
    q=np.interp(xnew,xold,p,left=p[0],right=p[-1])
    return normalize_nonneg(q)
```

`start_gauss_mix`: exponent maybe overflow; use `z=(x-mu)/sig; val=w*np.exp(np.clip(-0.5*z*z + skew*z, -50, 5))`. Clip high to avoid inf but preserves peaks. Good.

`start_spline`: heights exp normal; for interpolation. If knots length 2 only constant? fine.

`start_poly`: D=3, coefficients uniform [-2,2]; a=1+...; if all <=0, add floor.

Now, optimizer line search: We need current b? We compute sc,b. The candidate evaluation doesn't use b. Good.

In optimizer, if `best_candidate_sc < sc - 1e-12` accept. Use tolerance.

If no improvement and `it - last_improve > max(1500, 0.05*it)` reheat. But for early it small, 1500; for n=1024 maybe stall. Use `stall_threshold = 2000 if n<=256 else 8000` large n slower iterations? Actually iterations not dependent. Use 3000. If budget small, might not reheat. Fine.

After reheat, set `current`? Not until next loop.

if it % 5000 == 0: gamma = gamma_start + rng.random()*(gamma_end-gamma_start)
```
But gamma variable in loop determined by formula; we can let formula override. Not needed.

Maybe gamma should not increase unbounded if n large; gamma_end=300 relative. Good.

Now, final return: If best_p is None, create 128 uniform. The previous best should exist. But if all fail, uniform score2. Target not met but safe.

Let's ensure `evaluate_sequence` call exists only final. It might not be defined in this environment? They say assume imported. In try, if NameError, fallback.

```python
"""
Searches for a non-negative step sequence minimizing
    2*n*max(conv(a,a)) / sum(a)^2.

Method:
- The sequence is kept as a probability vector (sum = 1), so the score is
  2*n*max(conv(a,a)).
- The main local optimizer works in log-domain (softmax parameters) and
  minimizes a smoothed-max objective while also using a variance-flattening
  gradient.  Convolution windows and their gradients are computed with FFT.
- Multiple sequence lengths are tried, with diverse random / parametric
  initializations.
- A few LP-based directions are used as expensive mutations when available.
- The best sequence found before the deadline is returned.
"""

import numpy as np
import time
import operator

try:
    from scipy.optimize import linprog
    from scipy.sparse import csr_matrix
    HAVE_SCIPY = True
except Exception:
    HAVE_SCIPY = False


def full_conv(a):
    """Linear convolution a*a using FFT."""
    n = a.size
    if n == 0:
        return np.zeros(0, dtype=np.float64)
    if n == 1:
        return np.array([float(a[0]) * float(a[0])], dtype=np.float64)

    size = 2 * n - 1
    fsize = 1 << (size - 1).bit_length()
    fa = np.fft.rfft(a, fsize)
    b = np.fft.irfft(fa * fa, fsize)[:size]
    b = np.ascontiguousarray(b, dtype=np.float64)
    # Remove tiny numerical negatives.
    b = np.where(b > 0.0, b, 0.0)
    return b


def score_simplex(p):
    """Score assuming sum(p)=1."""
    p = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64)
    n = p.size
    if n <= 0:
        return np.inf
    b = full_conv(p)
    if b.size == 0:
        return np.inf
    return float(2.0 * n * np.max(b))


def score_and_b(p):
    """Return (score, convolution) assuming sum(p)=1."""
    p = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64)
    n = p.size
    b = full_conv(p)
    sc = float(2.0 * n * np.max(b)) if b.size else np.inf
    return sc, b


def normalize_nonneg(a):
    """Project to the nonnegative simplex."""
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    if a.size == 0:
        return np.array([], dtype=np.float64)
    a = np.where(np.isfinite(a), a, 0.0)
    a = np.where(a > 0.0, a, 0.0)
    s = float(np.sum(a))
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 1e-300:
        a = np.ones_like(a)
        s = float(a.size)
    return a / s


def project_simplex(v):
    """Euclidean projection onto the nonnegative simplex."""
    v = np.asarray(v, dtype=np.float64)
    n = v.size
    if n == 0:
        return v.copy()
    u = np.sort(v)[::-1]
    cssv = np.cumsum(u)
    idx = np.arange(1, n + 1, dtype=np.float64)
    mask = u * idx > (cssv - 1.0)
    if not np.any(mask):
        return np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64) / float(n)
    rho = int(np.where(mask)[0][-1])
    theta = (cssv[rho] - 1.0) / float(rho + 1)
    return np.where(v > theta, v - theta, 0.0)


def softmax_z(z):
    """Stable softmax."""
    z = np.asarray(z, dtype=np.float64)
    if z.size == 0:
        return z.copy()
    zm = z - np.max(z)
    zm = np.where(zm > -50.0, zm, -50.0)
    p = np.exp(zm)
    s = float(np.sum(p))
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 1e-300:
        p = np.ones_like(z, dtype=np.float64) / float(z.size)
    else:
        p = p / s
    return p


def interp_sequence(p, n):
    """Linearly resample a probability sequence to length n."""
    p = np.maximum(np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64), 0.0)
    if p.size == 0:
        return np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64) / float(max(1, n))
    if p.size == n:
        return normalize_nonneg(p)
    if p.size == 1:
        q = np.full(n, float(p[0]), dtype=np.float64)
        return normalize_nonneg(q)
    old = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, p.size)
    new = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n)
    q = np.interp(new, old, p, left=float(p[0]), right=float(p[-1]))
    return normalize_nonneg(q)


def correlate_valid_fft(w, p):
    """
    Compute c_i = sum_m p_m * w_{i+m}, i=0..n-1,
    where w has length 2n-1.  This is np.correlate(w, p, mode='valid')
    computed by FFT.
    """
    n = p.size
    if n == 0:
        return np.zeros(0, dtype=np.float64)
    if n == 1:
        return np.array([float(w[0]) * float(p[0])], dtype=np.float64)

    size = 2 * n - 1
    fsize = 1 << (size - 1).bit_length()
    fp = np.fft.rfft(p, fsize)
    fw = np.fft.rfft(w, fsize)
    c = np.fft.irfft(np.conj(fp) * fw, fsize)
    return c[:n]


def start_power(n, rng):
    x = (np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64) + 0.5) / float(n)
    alpha = float(rng.uniform(-0.5, 3.0))
    beta = float(rng.uniform(-0.5, 3.0))
    a = np.power(x + 1e-6, beta) * np.power(1.0 - x + 1e-6, alpha)
    a = np.maximum(a, 1e-6)
    return a


def start_sigmoid_mix(n, rng):
    x = (np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64) + 0.5) / float(n)
    a = np.full(n, 1.0, dtype=np.float64)
    count = int(rng.integers(1, 3))
    for _ in range(count):
        c = float(rng.uniform(0.1, 0.9))
        s = float(rng.uniform(0.05, 0.45))
        h = float(rng.uniform(0.5, 6.0))
        if rng.random() < 0.5:
            h = -h
        a += h / (1.0 + np.exp((x - c) / s))
    a = np.maximum(a, 1e-5)
    return a


def start_gauss_mix(n, rng):
    x = (np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64) + 0.5) / float(n)
    a = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
    count = int(rng.integers(1, 4))
    for _ in range(count):
        w = float(np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, 0.8)))
        mu = float(rng.beta(2.0, 2.0))
        sig = float(rng.uniform(0.05, 0.55))
        skew = float(rng.uniform(-3.0, 3.0))
        z = (x - mu) / sig
        e = -0.5 * z * z + skew * z
        e = np.where(e > -50.0, e, -50.0)
        e = np.where(e < 6.0, e, 6.0)
        a += w * np.exp(e)
    a += 1e-6
    return a


def start_fourier(n, rng):
    x = (np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64) + 0.5) / float(n)
    max_modes = min(14, max(1, n // 6))
    modes = int(rng.integers(2, max_modes + 1))
    z = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
    for m in range(1, modes + 1):
        amp1 = float(rng.normal(0.0, 1.2 / float(m + 0.5)))
        amp2 = float(rng.normal(0.0, 1.2 / float(m + 0.5)))
        ph1 = float(rng.uniform(0.0, 2.0 * np.pi))
        ph2 = float(rng.uniform(0.0, 2.0 * np.pi))
        z += amp1 * np.cos(2.0 * np.pi * m * x + ph1)
        z += amp2 * np.sin(2.0 * np.pi * m * x + ph2)
    z = z - np.mean(z)
    z = np.clip(z, -3.0, 3.0)
    return np.exp(0.9 * z)


def start_spline(n, rng):
    knots = np.concatenate((np.array([0.0, 1.0]), np.sort(rng.uniform(0.05, 0.95, size=4))))
    heights = np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, 1.0, size=knots.size))
    heights[0] *= 1.5
    xnew = (np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64) + 0.5) / float(n)
    a = np.interp(xnew, knots, heights)
    return np.maximum(a, 1e-6)


def start_poly(n, rng):
    x = (np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64) + 0.5) / float(n)
    a = np.full(n, 1.0, dtype=np.float64)
    d = int(rng.integers(2, 5))
    for p in range(1, d + 1):
        a += float(rng.uniform(-2.0, 2.0)) * np.power(x, p)
    a = np.maximum(a, 1e-5)
    return a


def start_random_smooth(n, rng):
    z = rng.standard_normal(n)
    zf = np.fft.rfft(z)
    keep = min(max(2, n // 10), zf.size)
    if keep < zf.size:
        zf[keep:] = 0.0
    z = np.fft.irfft(zf, n)
    z = z - np.mean(z)
    z = np.clip(z, -2.5, 2.5)
    return np.exp(0.8 * z)


def generate_starts(n, rng, count=40, prev=None):
    """
    Produce diverse nonnegative starts, mostly normalized probability vectors.
    """
    starts = []
    x = (np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64) + 0.5) / float(n)

    # Simple fixed starts.
    starts.append(np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64))
    starts.append(1.0 - x + 0.05)
    starts.append(x + 0.05)
    starts.append(np.maximum(1.0 / (0.4 + x), 1e-5))
    starts.append(np.maximum(1.0 / (0.4 + (1.0 - x)), 1e-5))

    # A few useful sigmoid starts.
    fixed_sig = [
        (0.25, 0.12, 3.0),
        (0.50, 0.18, -3.5),
        (0.65, 0.22, 2.5),
        (0.35, 0.35, -4.0),
        (0.80, 0.12, -2.0),
    ]
    for c, s, h in fixed_sig:
        a = 1.0 + h / (1.0 + np.exp((x - c) / s))
        starts.append(np.maximum(a, 1e-5))

    # Previous best, interpolated if needed.
    if prev is not None:
        p = np.asarray(prev, dtype=np.float64)
        if p.size > 0 and np.all(np.isfinite(p)):
            starts.append(interp_sequence(p, n))

    # Randomized parametric starts.
    types = 7
    for _ in range(count):
        t = int(rng.integers(0, types))
        if t == 0:
            starts.append(start_power(n, rng))
        elif t == 1:
            starts.append(start_sigmoid_mix(n, rng))
        elif t == 2:
            starts.append(start_gauss_mix(n, rng))
        elif t == 3:
            starts.append(start_fourier(n, rng))
        elif t == 4:
            starts.append(start_spline(n, rng))
        elif t == 5:
            starts.append(start_poly(n, rng))
        else:
            starts.append(start_random_smooth(n, rng))

    return starts


def optimize_logadam(p0, budget, rng, verbose=False):
    """
    Local optimizer in softmax/log-parameter space.

    It minimizes a smoothed maximum of conv(a,a) plus a variance-flattening
    term, using Adam-like normalized steps and line search.
    """
    p = normalize_nonneg(p0)
    n = p.size
    if n <= 1 or budget <= 0.0:
        sc = score_simplex(p)
        return p, sc

    z = np.log(np.maximum(p, 1e-300))
    z -= np.mean(z)

    m = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
    v = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
    b1 = 0.90
    b2 = 0.999
    eps = 1e-6

    if n <= 192:
        alphas = [1.0, 0.5, 0.25, 0.12, 0.06]
    elif n <= 768:
        alphas = [0.6, 0.3, 0.15, 0.08]
    else:
        alphas = [0.4, 0.2, 0.1]

    gamma_start = 4.0
    gamma_end = 260.0

    sc, b = score_and_b(p)
    best_p = p.copy()
    best_sc = sc
    z_best = z.copy()
    last_improve = 0
    it = 0

    t0 = time.monotonic()
    while True:
        if time.monotonic() - t0 >= budget:
            break
        it += 1

        if sc < best_sc - 1e-12:
            best_sc = sc
            best_p = p.copy()
            z_best = z.copy()
            last_improve = it
            if verbose and it % 1000 == 0:
                print(f"    logadam it={it} n={n} score={best_sc:.6f}")

        # Smooth peak weights: softmax over high b-values.
        max_b = float(np.max(b))
        gamma = gamma_start + (gamma_end - gamma_start) * min(1.0, float(it) / 25000.0)
        gamma *= 0.85 + 0.3 * float(rng.random())
        gamma = max(1.0, gamma)

        shift = gamma * (b - max_b)
        shift -= np.max(shift)
        shift = np.where(shift > -50.0, shift, -50.0)
        w = np.exp(shift)
        ws = float(np.sum(w))
        if ws <= 1e-300:
            w = np.zeros_like(b)
            w[int(np.argmax(b))] = 1.0
        else:
            w = w / ws

        # Gradient of score-like smoothed max.
        gp = 2.0 * float(n) * correlate_valid_fft(w, p)

        # Gradient of variance of b, used to flatten low coefficients too.
        mean_b = 1.0 / float(2 * n - 1)
        r = b - mean_b
        gv = 2.0 * float(n) * correlate_valid_fft(r, p)

        ngp = float(np.linalg.norm(gp))
        ngv = float(np.linalg.norm(gv))
        if ngp > 1e-12:
            gp /= ngp
        else:
            gp.fill(0.0)
        if ngv > 1e-12:
            gv /= ngv
        else:
            gv.fill(0.0)

        # More flattening when far from target; mostly peak-focus when close.
        if sc > 1.80:
            var_weight = 0.55
        elif sc > 1.60:
            var_weight = 0.40
        elif sc > 1.52:
            var_weight = 0.22
        else:
            var_weight = 0.05

        G = ((1.0 - var_weight) * gp + var_weight * gv)
        if rng.random() < 0.08:
            G += 0.15 * rng.standard_normal(n)

        pG = float(np.dot(p, G))
        zgrad = p * (G - pG)
        zgrad -= np.mean(zgrad)

        if float(np.max(np.abs(zgrad))) < 1e-14:
            # Gradient vanished; reheat later.
            if it - last_improve > 2500:
                scale = 0.05 + 0.15 * float(rng.random())
                z = z_best + scale * rng.standard_normal(n)
                z -= np.mean(z)
                p = softmax_z(z)
                m.fill(0.0)
                v.fill(0.0)
                sc, b = score_and_b(p)
                last_improve = it
            continue

        # Adam moments on z-gradient.
        m = b1 * m + (1.0 - b1) * zgrad
        v = b2 * v + (1.0 - b2) * (zgrad * zgrad)
        mh = m / (1.0 - b1 ** it)
        vh = v / (1.0 - b2 ** it)
        direction = mh / (np.sqrt(vh) + 1e-5)
        direction = np.where(np.abs(direction) < 3.0, direction, np.sign(direction) * 3.0)
        direction -= np.mean(direction)

        if float(np.max(np.abs(direction))) < 1e-12:
            continue

        # Line search over Adam-scaled log-moves.
        best_candidate_sc = sc
        best_candidate_z = z
        best_candidate_p = p
        for alpha in alphas:
            zn = z - alpha * direction
            zn -= np.mean(zn)
            pn = softmax_z(zn)
            scn = score_simplex(pn)
            if scn < best_candidate_sc - 1e-12:
                best_candidate_sc = scn
                best_candidate_z = zn
                best_candidate_p = pn

        accepted = False
        if best_candidate_sc < sc - 1e-12:
            z = best_candidate_z
            p = best_candidate_p
            sc = best_candidate_sc
            accepted = True
        else:
            # Mild annealing acceptance.
            if rng.random() < 0.025:
                alpha = alphas[-1]
                zn = z - alpha * direction
                zn -= np.mean(zn)
                pn = softmax_z(zn)
                scn = score_simplex(pn)
                if scn < sc * 1.012:
                    z = zn
                    p = pn
                    sc = scn
                    accepted = True

        if not accepted:
            # If stuck, restart from best.
            if it - last_improve > 3000:
                scale = 0.05 + 0.18 * float(rng.random())
                z = z_best + scale * rng.standard_normal(n)
                z -= np.mean(z)
                p = softmax_z(z)
                sc, b = score_and_b(p)
                m.fill(0.0)
                v.fill(0.0)
                last_improve = it

        # Refresh convolution for next gradient.
        if not accepted:
            sc, b = score_and_b(p)

        if it % 10000 == 0 and verbose:
            print(f"    logadam info n={n} it={it} current={sc:.6f} best={best_sc:.6f}")

    # Return the best point seen.
    return best_p, best_sc


def lp_direction(p):
    """
    Given p and M=max(p*p), find q>=0 maximizing sum(q) subject to p*q <= M.
    This is the LP direction from the previous state-of-the-art idea.
    """
    if not HAVE_SCIPY:
        return None
    p = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64)
    n = p.size
    if n < 16:
        return None

    # Strictly positive p makes the LP bounded.
    p_lp = p + 1e-9
    p_lp = normalize_nonneg(p_lp)
    _, b = score_and_b(p_lp)
    M = float(np.max(b)) + 1e-10

    idx = np.arange(n, dtype=np.int64)
    rows_parts = []
    cols_parts = []
    for j in range(n):
        rows_parts.append(idx + j)
        cols_parts.append(np.full(n, j, dtype=np.int64))
    rows = np.concatenate(rows_parts)
    cols = np.concatenate(cols_parts)
    vals = np.tile(p_lp, n)
    A = csr_matrix((vals, (rows, cols)), shape=(2 * n - 1, n))

    c = -np.ones(n)
    bounds = [(0.0, None)] * n

    try:
        res = linprog(
            c,
            A_ub=A,
            b_ub=np.full(2 * n - 1, M),
            bounds=bounds,
            method="highs",
            options={"presolve": True, "time_limit": 45},
        )
    except Exception:
        try:
            res = linprog(c, A_ub=A, b_ub=np.full(2 * n - 1, M), bounds=bounds)
        except Exception:
            return None

    if res is None or getattr(res, "status", 1) != 0:
        return None
    q = getattr(res, "x", None)
    if q is None:
        return None
    q = np.asarray(q, dtype=np.float64)
    q = np.where(np.isfinite(q) and q > 0.0, q, 0.0)
    s = float(np.sum(q))
    if s <= 1e-10:
        return None
    return q / s


def try_lp_polish(p, budget, rng):
    """Use LP directions as mutations, followed by short log-Adam polishing."""
    p = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64)
    if p.size < 16 or not HAVE_SCIPY or budget <= 5.0:
        return p, score_simplex(p)

    best_p = normalize_nonneg(p)
    best_sc = score_simplex(best_p)
    t0 = time.monotonic()
    attempts = 0

    while time.monotonic() - t0 < budget and attempts < 4:
        attempts += 1
        q = lp_direction(best_p)
        if q is None:
            break

        t_candidates = [0.08, 0.18, 0.32, 0.50, 0.72]
        improved = False
        for t in t_candidates:
            cand = normalize_nonneg((1.0 - t) * best_p + t * q)
            sc = score_simplex(cand)
            if sc < best_sc - 1e-12:
                best_sc = sc
                best_p = cand
                improved = True
        # Also try pure q.
        qn = normalize_nonneg(q)
        sc = score_simplex(qn)
        if sc < best_sc - 1e-12:
            best_sc = sc
            best_p = qn
            improved = True

        if improved and time.monotonic() - t0 < budget - 20.0:
            rem = max(1.0, min(60.0, (budget - (time.monotonic() - t0)) * 0.5))
            best_p, best_sc = optimize_logadam(best_p, rem, rng)

    return best_p, best_sc


def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=2400.0):
    """
    Return a nonnegative sequence expected to have a low evaluation score.
    """
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    deadline = time.monotonic() + max(1.0, float(budget_s) - 12.0)

    best_p = None
    best_sc = np.inf

    # Start from the known construction if it exists.
    try:
        prev = np.asarray(height_sequence_1, dtype=np.float64)
        prev = np.clip(np.where(np.isfinite(prev), prev, 0.0), 0.0, 1000.0)
        if prev.ndim == 1 and prev.size >= 8 and np.sum(prev) > 0.01:
            p = normalize_nonneg(prev)
            sc = score_simplex(p)
            if np.isfinite(sc):
                best_p = p
                best_sc = sc
                print(f"[init] previous best n={p.size} score={sc:.6f}")
    except Exception:
        pass

    if best_p is None:
        best_p = np.ones(128, dtype=np.float64) / 128.0
        best_sc = score_simplex(best_p)
        print(f"[init] fallback uniform n=128 score={best_sc:.6f}")

    total_time = max(1.0, deadline - time.monotonic())

    # Sequence length plans: (n, fraction of time, number of starts, max opt starts)
    plans = [
        (128, 0.18, 70, 10),
        (256, 0.27, 55, 8),
        (512, 0.30, 45, 6),
        (1024, 0.25, 28, 4),
    ]

    for plan_idx, (n, frac, start_count, opt_count) in enumerate(plans):
        now = time.monotonic()
        if now >= deadline:
            break

        # Allow the last plan to consume leftover time if useful.
        remaining_total = deadline - now
        plan_time = frac * total_time
        if plan_idx == len(plans) - 1:
            plan_time = remaining_total * 0.85
        plan_time = max(20.0, min(plan_time, remaining_total * 0.85))
        if remaining_total < 30.0:
            break

        print(f"[plan] n={n} allocated={plan_time:.1f}s")

        starts = generate_starts(n, rng, count=start_count, prev=best_p)

        # Score all starts.
        scored = []
        for a in starts:
            p = normalize_nonneg(a)
            if p.size != n:
                continue
            sc = score_simplex(p)
            if np.isfinite(sc):
                scored.append((sc, p))

        if not scored:
            scored = [(score_simplex(np.ones(n) / float(n)), np.ones(n) / float(n))]

        scored.sort(key=operator.itemgetter(0))

        # Choose a mix of top and diverse candidates.
        selected = []
        top = min(opt_count, len(scored))
        for sc, p in scored[:top]:
            selected.append(p)

        if len(scored) > len(selected):
            rcount = min(max(1, opt_count // 2), len(scored) - len(selected))
            extra = len(scored) - len(selected)
            idxs = rng.choice(extra, size=rcount, replace=False)
            for ii in idxs:
                selected.append(scored[len(selected) + int(ii)][1])

        # Remove exact duplicates approximately: just limit count.
        selected = selected[: max(1, opt_count)]

        # If plan_time is large, slightly favor the best start by giving it more budget.
        budgets = [plan_time / float(len(selected))] * len(selected)
        if len(selected) >= 2:
            budgets[0] = plan_time * 0.25
            rem = plan_time - budgets[0]
            if rem > 0.0:
                budgets[1:] = [rem / float(max(1, len(budgets) - 1))] * (len(budgets) - 1)

        for k, p_init in enumerate(selected):
            now = time.monotonic()
            tb = min(float(budgets[k]), max(0.0, deadline - now - 5.0))
            if tb < 2.0:
                break
            p_opt, sc_opt = optimize_logadam(p_init, tb, rng)
            if sc_opt < best_sc - 1e-12:
                best_sc = sc_opt
                best_p = p_opt
                print(f"[n={n} start={k}] new best {best_sc:.6f}")
            else:
                print(f"[n={n} start={k}] score={sc_opt:.6f} best={best_sc:.6f}")

    # Final polishing phase.
    now = time.monotonic()
    remain = max(0.0, deadline - now - 3.0)
    if best_p is None:
        best_p = np.ones(128, dtype=np.float64) / 128.0
        best_sc = score_simplex(best_p)

    print(f"[final polish] n={best_p.size} score={best_sc:.6f} remain={remain:.1f}s")

    if remain > 30.0 and np.isfinite(best_sc):
        work = best_p

        # If currently at a short length and still far from target, try a longer
        # interpolation as a new manifold.
        if best_p.size <= 192 and best_sc > 1.52 and remain > 180.0:
            up = interp_sequence(best_p, 512)
            sc_up = score_simplex(up)
            print(f"[upsample] to 512 initial score={sc_up:.6f}")
            up_opt, sc_up_opt = optimize_logadam(up, min(remain * 0.45, 700.0), rng)
            if sc_up_opt < best_sc - 1e-12:
                best_sc = sc_up_opt
                best_p = up_opt
                print(f"[upsample] improved best to {best_sc:.6f}")

            now = time.monotonic()
            remain = max(0.0, deadline - now - 3.0)

        if remain > 25.0:
            # Long log-Adam polish on the current best.
            t1 = min(remain * 0.55, 900.0)
            p2, sc2 = optimize_logadam(best_p, t1, rng)
            if sc2 < best_sc - 1e-12:
                best_sc = sc2
                best_p = p2
                print(f"[polish] logadam best {best_sc:.6f}")

            now = time.monotonic()
            remain = max(0.0, deadline - now - 3.0)

            # LP mutations for smallish lengths.
            if best_p.size <= 256 and remain > 60.0 and HAVE_SCIPY:
                t2 = min(remain * 0.40, 360.0)
                p3, sc3 = try_lp_polish(best_p, t2, rng)
                if sc3 < best_sc - 1e-12:
                    best_sc = sc3
                    best_p = p3
                    print(f"[polish] LP best {best_sc:.6f}")

                now = time.monotonic()
                remain = max(0.0, deadline - now - 3.0)

            if remain > 15.0:
                t3 = min(remain * 0.6, 240.0)
                p4, sc4 = optimize_logadam(best_p, t3, rng)
                if sc4 < best_sc - 1e-12:
                    best_sc = sc4
                    best_p = p4
                    print(f"[polish2] logadam best {best_sc:.6f}")

    # Very short emergency polish if anything remains.
    now = time.monotonic()
    remain = max(0.0, deadline - now - 1.5)
    if remain > 2.0:
        p5, sc5 = optimize_logadam(best_p, remain, rng)
        if sc5 < best_sc - 1e-12:
            best_sc = sc5
            best_p = p5

    # Final output.
    result_np = np.maximum(np.asarray(best_p, dtype=np.float64), 0.0)
    result_np = np.where(np.isfinite(result_np), result_np, 0.0)
    s = float(np.sum(result_np))
    if s <= 1e-10:
        result_np = np.ones(128, dtype=np.float64) / 128.0
    else:
        result_np = result_np / s

    result = [float(x) for x in result_np]

    try:
        eval_sc = float(evaluate_sequence(result))
        print(f"[final] length={len(result)} internal={best_sc:.6f} evaluator={eval_sc:.6f}")
        if (not np.isfinite(eval_sc)) or eval_sc > max(2.5, best_sc * 1.5):
            fallback = [1.0] * 128
            return fallback
    except Exception as e:
        print(f"[final] evaluator not available: {e}")

    return result
```
